-linear isomorphism (
6.14.7
) in
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
for a weaker topology. Hence it is an isomorphism of

{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
.

Similarly, by Corollary
3.13
, we may dualize (both weakly and strongly) the
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
-isomorphism (
5.24.3
) and,
for
!=\emptyset,\circ
, we obtain isomorphisms

(6.14.9)

{\mathcal{L}}^{(X,Y)}_{\mathscr{D}}:{\mathscr{D}}^{!}(X\times Y,k)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }{\mathscr{D}}^{!}(X,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}^{!}(Y,k)\;.

Finally, by Lemma
3.12
we may dualize (both weakly and strongly) the injective morphism (
5.24.6
) to obtain, for
!=\emptyset,\circ
, a morphism

(6.14.10)

{\mathcal{L}}^{(X,Y)}_{\mathscr{D}}:{\mathscr{D}}^{!}_{\rm unif}(X\times Y,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }{\mathscr{D}}^{!}_{\rm unif}(X,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}^{!}_{\rm unif}(Y,k)\;.

We summarize what we have proven.

Proposition 6.15
.

Notation as in Remark
5.26
.

1.

For
X
a
td
-space, the product morphism

(6.15.1)

\mu^{(X)}:{\mathscr{C}}_{?}(X,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{C}}_{?}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ {\mathcal{L}}^{(X,X)}\ }{\mathscr{C}}_{?}(X\times X,k)\xrightarrow{\ \Delta_{X}^{\ast}\ }{\mathscr{C}}_{?}(X,k)

of
loc.cit.
, for
?=\emptyset,{\rm acs}
weakly (resp. strongly) transposes to a co-associative and co-commutative coproduct for
!={\rm acs},\emptyset
, respectively,

(6.15.2)

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}:{\mathscr{D}}_{!}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ (\Delta_{X}^{\ast})^{\bf t}\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{!}(X\times X,k)\xrightarrow{\ {\mathcal{L}}^{(X,X)}_{\mathscr{D}}\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{!}(X,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{!}(X,k)

(resp.

(6.15.3)

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}:{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{!}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ (\Delta_{X}^{\ast})^{\bf t}\ }{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{!}(X\times X,k)\xrightarrow{\ {\mathcal{L}}^{(X,X)}_{\mathscr{D}}\ }{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{!}(X,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{!}(X,k)\;\mbox{)}

such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}(\delta_{x})=\delta_{x}\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}\delta_{x}
, for any
x\in X
.
In case
!={\rm acs}
, the coproduct
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}
of (
6.15.2
) (resp. of (
6.15.3
)) together with the augmentation

\varepsilon_{\mathscr{D}}^{(X)}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }k\;\;,\;\;\mu\longmapsto\mu(X)

(resp.

\varepsilon_{\mathscr{D}}^{(X)}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}^{\circ}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }k\;\;,\;\;\mu\longmapsto\mu(X)\;\mbox{)}\;,

determine a
k
-coalgebra structure on the object
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}^{\circ}(X,k)
) of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
(resp. of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
). In case
!=\emptyset
, the coproduct
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}
still exists but
fails to define a
k
-coalgebra structure on
{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)
(resp. on
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}(X,k)
), for lack of an augmentation.

2.

Let
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
be a neat
td
-uniform space,
as in (
4.28.1
). Then the product morphism (
6.15.1
), for
?={\rm unif}
, weakly (resp. strongly) transposes to the co-associative and co-commutative coproduct (
6.15.2
) for
!={\rm unif}
, such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}(\delta_{x})=\delta_{x}\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}\delta_{x}
, for any
x\in X
. That coproduct, together with the augmentation

\varepsilon_{\mathscr{D}}^{(X)}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }k\;\;,\;\;\mu\longmapsto\mu(X)

(resp.

\varepsilon_{\mathscr{D}}^{(X)}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}^{\circ}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }k\;\;,\;\;\mu\longmapsto\mu(X)\;\mbox{)}\;,

determine a
k
-coalgebra structure on the object
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(X,k)
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}^{\circ}(X,k)
) of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
(resp. of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
).

6.5
Summary of functions vs. measures duality

We summarize the essentials of what we have proven in section
6
.

Corollary 6.16
.

Let
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
be a neat
td
-uniform space,
as in (
4.28.1
).

There is a natural sequence

(6.16.1)

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}(X,k)

of associative and commutative (non-unital)
k
-algebra objects of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
. The second morphism
is a morphism of unital
k
-algebras, as well. The first morphism of the sequence (
6.16.1
)
is a kernel in the category

{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
, all morphisms are injective and the composite morphism has
dense set-theoretic image.

The weak (resp. strong) dual of the sequence (
6.16.1
)
is a sequence

(6.16.2)

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ {\rm inc}\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)

(resp.

(6.16.3)

{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\ {\rm inc}\ }{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}(X,k)\;\mbox{)}

of coassociative and cocommutative (non-counital)
k
-coalgebra objects of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
(resp. of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
). All morphisms are injective and have dense set-theoretic image;
the morphism
{\rm inc}
is a morphism of counital
k
-coalgebras, as well.

The sequence (
6.16.1
) may be reconstructed from (
6.16.2
) via

(6.16.4)

\begin{split}{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)={\mathscr{D}}(X,k)^{\prime}_{\rm weak}&={\mathscr{D}}(X,k)^{\prime}_{\rm strong}\longrightarrow\\
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)&:={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)^{\prime}_{\rm strong}\\
\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}(X,k)&={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)^{\prime}_{\rm weak}\end{split}

By (
6.9.1
)
and the first line of
(
6.11.7
), we have both

(6.16.5)

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)={\rm colim}^{\rm u}_{C\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)}({\mathscr{D}}(C,k),j_{C,C^{\prime}})={\rm lim}_{D\in{\mathscr{Q}}(X)}({\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(D,k),h_{D,D^{\prime}})\;.

By (
6.16.6
) and the second line of (
6.11.7
), we have both

(6.16.6)

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}^{\circ}(X,k)={\rm colim}^{\rm u}_{C\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)}({\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}(C,k),j_{C,C^{\prime}})={\rm lim}^{\rm can}_{D\in{\mathscr{Q}}(X)}({\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(D,k),h_{D,D^{\prime}})

in
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
.

From the discussion we have obtained

Corollary 6.17
.

For any
td
-space
X
, we have
weak perfect pairings

(6.17.1)

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)\longrightarrow k

and

(6.17.2)

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}(X,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\longrightarrow k

We also have strong
perfect pairings

(6.17.3)

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}(X,k)\longrightarrow k

and

(6.17.4)

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}^{\circ}(X,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\longrightarrow k\;.

For any uniform
td
space
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
we have a strong-left and weak-right perfect pairing

(6.17.5)

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\times{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\longrightarrow k\;.

Any of those pairings will be denoted by

(f,\mu)\longmapsto f\circ\mu=:\int_{X}f(x)\mu(x)

and will be called
integration over
X
.

The previous integration pairings are compatible in an obvious sense with the sequence (
6.16.4
).

7
Measures: direct definition

Definition 7.1
.

Let
X
(resp.
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
) be an object of
td-{\mathcal{S}}
(resp. a neat object of
td-{\mathcal{U}}
).
We let
\Sigma(X)
(resp.
\Sigma_{D}(X)
for
D\in{\mathscr{J}}
) be the boolean ring
of subsets
U\subset X
which are
clopen
,
i.e.
both open and closed,
(resp. of the form
U=\pi_{D}^{-1}(V)
for
V\subset D
) in
X
.
We regard the multiplicative monoid
(\Sigma(X),\cdot=\cap)

(resp.
({\mathcal{K}}(X),\cdot=\cap)
) as a multiplicative submonoid of

{\mathscr{C}}(X,k)
(resp. of
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
) via
U\longmapsto\chi^{(X)}_{U}
. These embeddings extend to inclusions of
k
-algebras on the previous multiplicative monoids

(7.1.1)

k[{\mathcal{K}}(X)]\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\;\;,\;\;k[\Sigma(X)]\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}(X,k)\;.

If
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
is given, we set
\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X):=\bigcup_{D\in{\mathscr{J}}}\Sigma_{D}(X)
and call its elements
uniformly measurable subsets of
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
.
For any
D\in{\mathscr{J}}
we have inclusions of rings
\Sigma_{D}(X)\leq\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X)\leq\Sigma(X)
.
Then
(\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X),\cdot)
is a multiplicative submonoid of

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}(X,k)
and
U\longmapsto\chi^{(X)}_{U}

extends to inclusions of
k
-algebras (
5.4.6
)

(7.1.2)

k[\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X)]\longrightarrow\bigcup_{D\in{\mathscr{J}}}{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}(D,k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\;.

The following proposition provides a more explicit alternative definition of the three spaces of measures which appear in (
6.16.2
).

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
X
(resp.
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
) be an object of
td-{\mathcal{S}}
(resp. of
td-{\mathcal{U}}
).
Then,

1.

the inclusion
k[\Sigma(X)]\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}(X,k)

of (
7.1.1
)
is dense;

2.

the topological
k
-coalgebra
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)
of uniform
k
-valued measures
on
(X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
, coincides with the set of maps

(7.2.1)

\mu:\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X)\longrightarrow k

such that

(UNIFMEAS)

For any
D\in{\mathscr{J}}
and any disjoint subfamily
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
of
\Sigma_{D}(X)
,
the
A
-series
\sum_{\alpha\in A}\mu(U_{\alpha})

converges unconditionally to
\mu(\bigcup_{\alpha\in A}U_{\alpha})
.

The map (
7.2.1
) corresponding to
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)={\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)^{\prime}_{\rm weak}
is given
by the formula

(7.2.2)

U\longmapsto\mu(U):=\chi_{U}^{(X)}\circ\mu=\int_{X}\chi_{U}^{(X)}(x)\mu(x)\;.

Conversely, any map (
7.2.1
) satisfying
(UNIFMEAS)
above
extends uniquely to an element of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)
.
The coproduct

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)\widehat{\otimes}_{k}^{\rm u}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)

of (
6.15.2
)
is given
by

(7.2.3)

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathscr{D}}}^{(X)}(\mu)(U\times V)=\mu(U\cap V)\;,

for any
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)
, and
U,V\in\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X)
.
The topology of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)
has a basis of open submodules

{\mathscr{U}}_{D,J}

indexed by ideals
J\in{\mathcal{P}}(k)
and discrete quotients of
X

\pi_{D}:X\to D
in
{\mathscr{J}}
,
where

(7.2.4)

{\mathscr{U}}_{D,J}=\{\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(X,k)\,|\,\mu(U)\in J\,,\forall\,U\in\Sigma_{D}(X)\,\}\;.

3.

the topological
k
-coalgebra
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)={\mathscr{C}}(X,k)^{\prime}_{\rm weak}
(
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm acs}(X,k)={\mathscr{C}}(X,k)^{\prime}_{\rm strong}
) of
k
-valued measures with almost compact support
on
X
coincides, via the correspondence (
7.2.2
), with the
k
-module
of
maps

(7.2.5)

\mu:\Sigma(X)\longrightarrow k

such that

(ACSMEAS)

For any disjoint subfamily
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
of
\Sigma(X)
such that

U:=\bigcup_{\alpha\in A}U_{\alpha}\in\Sigma(X)
,
the
A
-series
\sum_{\alpha\in A}\mu(U_{\alpha})

converges unconditionally to
\mu(U)
in
k
.

The topology of
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
is the naive
k
-canonical topology. The topology of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)

may be described as follows.
For any
td
-partition
{\mathscr{P}}\in{\mathscr{Q}}(X)
of
X
and any
J\in{\mathcal{P}}(k)
, we set

V_{{\mathscr{P}},J}:=\{\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\,|\,\mu(C)\in J\;,\;\forall\;C\in{\mathscr{P}}\,\}\;.

Then,
V_{{\mathscr{P}},J}
is a
k
-submodule of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
, and the family
\{V_{{\mathscr{P}},J}\}_{{\mathscr{P}},J}
, indexed by
{\mathscr{Q}}(X)\times{\mathcal{P}}(k)
, is a basis of open
k
-submodules of

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
.

The coproducts of (
6.15.2
) and (
6.15.3
) are given by formula
(
7.2.3
), for any
U,V\in\Sigma(X)
.

4.

The topological
k
-coalgebra
{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}(X,k)
) of
k
-valued measures on
X

coincides with the space of maps

(7.2.6)

\mu:{\mathcal{K}}(X)\longrightarrow k

as in (
7.2.2
)
such that

(MEAS)

For any finite disjoint subfamily
{\mathscr{F}}\subset{\mathcal{K}}(X)
and for

U:=\bigcup_{C\in{\mathscr{F}}}C
,
\sum_{C\in{\mathscr{F}}}\mu(C)=\mu(U)
.

The coproduct of (
6.15.2
)
is given by formula
(
7.2.3
), for any
U,V\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)
. A basis of open
k
-modules for the
k
-linear topology of
{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)
is the family
\{U_{{\mathscr{F}};I}\}_{{\mathscr{F}};I}
, indexed by finite families
{\mathscr{F}}\subset{\mathcal{K}}(X)
and
I\in{\mathcal{P}}(k)
, where

(7.2.7)

U_{{\mathscr{F}},I}:=\{\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)\,|\,\mu(C)\in I\;,\;\forall\;C\in{\mathscr{F}}\,\}\;.

The topology of
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}(X,k)
is the naive canonical topology.

5.

The first morphism of the sequence (
6.16.2
)
is induced by the restriction of
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
,
\mu:\Sigma(X)\longrightarrow k
, to
\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X)
. The second morphism takes
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)
to the measure

(7.2.8)

\widehat{\mu}:{\mathcal{K}}(X)\longrightarrow k\;\;,\;\;C\longmapsto\int_{X}\chi_{C}^{(X)}\mu\;,

using the fact (see
\mathit{3}
of Lemma
5.5
) that
\chi_{C}^{(X)}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}((X,\Theta_{\mathscr{J}}),k)
.

We omit the proof, since it is quite standard

Proposition 7.3
.

Let

Res:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)

be the composite morphism in the sequence (
6.16.2
).
The set-theoretic image of
Res
is the set of measures
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)
such that

(ACSMEASbis)

For any
I\in{\mathcal{P}}(k)
, there exists
C_{I}\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)
such that, for any
V\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)
,
V\cap C_{I}=\emptyset
implies

\mu(V)\in I
.

Proof.

Assume
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
but
Res(\mu)
does not satisfy
(ACSMEASbis)
for some
I\in{\mathcal{P}}(k)
. Then
for any
td
-partition
{\mathscr{P}}

of
X
,
\mu(C)\notin I
, for an infinite subset of
C\in{\mathscr{P}}
. So, there is a sequence
\{C_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
{\mathcal{K}}(X)
such that

1.

\mu(C_{i})\notin I
, for any
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

2.

U:=\bigcup_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}C_{i}\in\Sigma(X)
;

3.

\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}\mu(C_{i})
does not converge unconditionally.

This is absurd, since
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)
.

Conversely, let
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}(X,k)
and assume
\mu
satisfies

(ACSMEASbis)
. We extend
\mu
to
\overline{\mu}:\Sigma(X)\longrightarrow k
as follows. For any
U\in\Sigma(X)
,
U
is a
td
-space. Therefore
U
admits a
td
-partition
{\mathscr{P}}
. The
{\mathbb{P}}
-series
\sum_{C\in{\mathscr{P}}}\mu(C)

is unconditionally convergent. We then set

\overline{\mu}(U):=\sum_{C\in{\mathscr{P}}}\mu(C)\;.

We check that, if
{\mathscr{Q}}
is another
td
-partition of
U
,

(7.3.1)

\sum_{C\in{\mathscr{P}}}\mu(C)=\sum_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{Q}}}\mu(C^{\prime})\;.

In fact, the open compact subsets
C\cap C^{\prime}
form an open compact partition
{\mathscr{P}}\cap{\mathscr{Q}}
of
U
which refines the previous ones. We may then assume that
{\mathscr{P}}
is a refinement of
{\mathscr{Q}}
, in which case formula (
7.3.1
) is clear. This gives a well-defined extension
\overline{\mu}
of
\mu
to
\Sigma(X)
. Finally,
\overline{\mu}
satisfies
(ACSMEASbis)
by construction.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

In contrast to point
\mathit{1}
of Proposition
7.2
,
k[{\mathcal{K}}(X)]
(resp.
k[\Sigma_{\mathscr{J}}(X)]
) is not in general dense via
U\longmapsto\chi^{(X)}_{U}
in
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(X,k)

(resp. in
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}(X,k)
).

In fact, let us assume that
X=D
is discrete (and endowed with the discrete uniformity) and that
k
is discrete. Then

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}(D,k)={\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}(D,k)=(\prod_{y\in D}k\chi_{y}^{(D)})^{\rm dis\,}

and the density statement would imply the equality

k[{\mathfrak{P}}(D)]=(\prod_{y\in D}k\chi_{y}^{(D)})^{\rm dis\,}

which is clearly false if
D
is infinite.

8
Commutative
td
-groups

8.1
td
-groups

We specialize here our previous results to group objects of the category of
td
-spaces. Second countable such groups are already of high interest
[
27
]
,
[
28
]
.
A proof of the next two lemmas can easily derived from
[
17
, §2]

and goes back to David van Dantzig around 1930.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
G=(G,\cdot)
be a topological group.
The following conditions on
G
are equivalent.

1.

G
is a
td
-space;

2.

G
admits a basis of neighborhoods of
1_{G}
consisting of compact open subgroups;

3.

G
admits an open profinite subgroup.

Proof.

We observe that (2)
\Rightarrow
(1) because if
H
is a compact open subgroup of
G
, then
H
is a Stone space and the partition of
G
in left
H
-cosets shows that
G
is a sum of Stone spaces. So, Theorem
4.12
shows that
G
is a
td
-space. Now, clearly (3)
\Rightarrow
(2). To prove (1)
\Rightarrow
(3) (which is the only non-trivial part) we may follow the proof of
[
17
, §2]
.
∎

Definition 8.2
.

A
td
-group
is a topological group
G
satisfying the equivalent conditions of Lemma
8.1
.

Lemma 8.3
.

A compact
td
-group is a profinite group, and conversely.

Part
\mathit{2}
of Lemma
8.1
indicates that we may restrict to topological groups
G
endowed with a
group topology
, namely for which
the family
{\mathcal{P}}(G)
of open subgroups is a basis of neighborhoods of
1_{G}
. Then, the
left uniformity
on
G
is the uniformity for which a basis of uniform covers is given by partitions

G/P=\{gP\,|\,g\in G\,\}

in left cosets for any given open subgroup
P\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)
; left multiplications are uniform automorphisms of the

left uniform group

(G,\{G/P\}_{P\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)})
.
Similarly for the
right uniformity
on
G
, partitions
P\backslash G
, and right multiplications. The map
x\mapsto x^{-1}
is a uniform antiisomorphism

(G,\{P\backslash G\}_{P\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(G,\{G/P\}_{P\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)})\;.

Our category of topological groups admits limits and colimits.
A topological group
G
is separated if and only if
\bigcap_{H\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)}H=\{1_{G}\}
. The left uniform group
G
is complete if and only if, as a uniform space,

G={\rm lim}_{H\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)}G/H
, where the set of left cosets
G/H
is given the discrete uniformity. Equivalently, the right uniform group
G
is complete if and only if
G={\rm lim}_{H\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)}H\backslash G
, where
H\backslash G
is given the discrete uniformity. So
G
is complete for the left uniformity if and only if it is so for the right one. We then say that the topological group
G
is
complete
; this implies that
G
is separated. By default, we will equip a topological group with its left uniformity.
By Lemma
4.32

any
td
-group is complete.

8.2
Hopf algebra structures on commutative
td
-groups

From now on we will deal exclusively with commutative
td
-groups.

On such a group the left and right uniformities coincide, and will be understood. We will use the name of
ball of radius
H
for any coset
gH
, for
g\in G
and
H\in{\mathcal{P}}(G)
.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
G
be a commutative
td
-group.

1.

{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)
is
a Hopf algebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
.
The weak dual

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)
of
{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)

is a Hopf algebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
. For
\mu,\nu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)
, and
U\in\Sigma(G)
,

(8.4.1)

\mu_{\mathscr{D}}(\mu\otimes\nu)(U)=(\mu\times\nu)(\{(g,h)\in G\times G\,|\,gh\in U\,\})\;.

The weak dual of the Hopf
k
-algebra object
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)
of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
is the Hopf
k
-algebra object
{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)
of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
. The strong dual of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)
is the Hopf
k
-algebra object
{\mathscr{C}}^{\circ}(G,k)={\mathscr{C}}(G,k)^{\rm can}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
.

2.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
) is a Hopf
k
-algebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
(resp. of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
).
The
k
-algebra object
{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
is the strong dual of both
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
and
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
. It is a Hopf
k
-algebra object of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
.

3.

We have a weak perfect duality pairing of Hopf
k
-algebra objects of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\longrightarrow k\;,

a left-strong right-weak perfect duality pairing of Hopf
k
-algebra objects of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\longrightarrow k\;,

and a strong perfect duality pairing of Hopf
k
-algebra objects of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\longrightarrow k\;.

Any of the previous pairings is denoted by

(f,\mu)\longmapsto f\circ\mu=\int_{G}f(x)\mu(x)\;,

and called
integration pairing
over
G
. The product of measures
\mu_{G}(\mu\otimes\nu)=:\mu\nu
is called
convolution
and may be described
as

\begin{split}\int_{G}f(x)d(\mu\nu)(x)=(\mu\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}\nu)&\circ{\mathbb{P}}_{G}(f)=\int_{G\times G}{\mathbb{P}}_{G}(f)(x,y)\;d(\mu\times\nu)(x,y)=\\
&\int_{G\times G}f(xy)d\mu(x)d\nu(y)\;.\end{split}

Proof.

\mathit{1}.
The map
\Delta_{G}^{\ast}:f(x)\longmapsto f(xy)
induces, via the
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
-isomorphism (
5.24.4
), a coproduct

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathscr{C}}:{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)\xrightarrow{\ \Delta_{G}^{\ast}\ }{\mathscr{C}}(G\times G,k)\xrightarrow{\ ({\mathcal{L}}^{(G,G)})^{-1}\ }{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)

which, together with the counit map

\varepsilon_{\mathscr{C}}:{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)\longrightarrow k

such that
\varepsilon_{\mathscr{C}}(f)=f(1_{G})
, and the inversion
\rho_{\mathscr{C}}(f)(x)=f(x^{-1})
, makes

{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)=({\mathscr{C}}(G,k),\mu_{\mathscr{C}},1_{\mathscr{C}},{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathscr{C}},\varepsilon_{\mathscr{C}},\rho_{\mathscr{C}})

a Hopf algebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
. By weak transposition we obtain, via the
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
-isomorphism (
6.14.8
)

{\mathcal{L}}^{(G,G)}_{\mathscr{D}}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G\times G,k)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\;,

a product map

\mu_{\mathscr{D}}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\xrightarrow{\ ({\mathcal{L}}_{\mathscr{D}}^{(G,G)})^{-1}\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G\times G,k)\xrightarrow{\ (\Delta_{G}^{\ast})^{\bf t}\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\;,

which, together with the unit map

1_{\mathscr{D}}:k\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\;,\;a\longmapsto a\delta_{1_{G}}\;,

and the inversion
\rho_{\mathscr{D}}(\mu)(U)=\mu(U^{-1})
, where for any
U\in\Sigma(G)
,
U^{-1}=\{g^{-1}\}_{g\in U}
, makes

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)=({\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k),\mu_{\mathscr{D}},1_{\mathscr{D}},{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathscr{D}},\varepsilon_{\mathscr{D}},\rho_{\mathscr{D}})

a Hopf algebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
.

Formula (
8.4.1
) is easily checked.
The rest of comma
\mathit{1}
is clear.

\mathit{2}
.
We know from
\mathit{2}
of Proposition
6.15
that
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
) is a counital
k
-coalgebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
(resp. of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
). We show it carries a
k
-algebra structure, as well. For any
H\in{\mathcal{K}}(G)
,
G/H
is a discrete group and the
construction of the previous point gives to
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k)={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G/H,k)
the structure of Hopf
k
-algebra object of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
. In particular, there are product morphisms

\mu_{{\mathscr{D}},H}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k)\;.

By (
6.13.5
)

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)={\rm lim}_{H\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G/H,k)

(resp. by (
6.13.6
)

{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}(G,k)={\rm lim}^{\rm can}_{H\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G/H,k)\in{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}\;\mbox{)}\;.

This shows that

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)^{\prime}_{\rm strong}={\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}(G,k)^{\prime}_{\rm strong}={\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)

so that, by (
3.11.1
) for
?={\rm strong}
,

From (
2.14.2
) we deduce the existence of a natural morphism

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }{\rm lim}_{H\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)}({\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k))\;.

The product morphisms
\mu_{{\mathscr{D}},H}
induce
a morphism

{\rm lim}_{H\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)}({\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k))\xrightarrow{\ \ }{\rm lim}_{H\in{\mathcal{K}}(X)}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G/H,k)

hence all in all a morphism

\mu_{\mathscr{D}}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm u}_{k}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\xrightarrow{\ \ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\;.

We conclude that
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
is a
k
-algebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
.

Then, using (
6.14.10
), we conclude that
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
) is a Hopf
k
-algebra object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{k}
(resp. of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
).
Now,
{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
is the strong dual of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
, and by (
3.11.1
) for
?={\rm strong}
, it is a Hopf
k
-algebra object of
{{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}}^{\rm can}_{k}
, too.
∎

8.3
Invariant endomorphisms

We recall in this section some properties of duality between algebra and coalgebra objects in a monoidal category of (topological)
k
-modules. We will not list precisely the assumptions needed and will leave to the reader the task of verifying that our statements hold true in the applications we present.

Let
(R,{\mathbb{P}}_{R},\varepsilon_{R})
be any cocommutative
k
-coalgebra. An element
h\in{\rm End}_{k}(R)
is
invariant
if

(8.4.2)

{\mathbb{P}}_{R}h=({\rm id}_{R}\otimes h){\mathbb{P}}_{R}=(h\otimes{\rm id}_{R}){\mathbb{P}}_{R}\;.

In particular, by composition to the left with
{\rm id}_{R}\otimes\varepsilon_{R}
or with
\varepsilon_{R}\otimes{\rm id}_{R}
,

(8.4.3)

h=({\rm id}_{R}\otimes\varepsilon_{R}h){\mathbb{P}}_{R}=(\varepsilon_{R}h\otimes{\rm id}_{R}){\mathbb{P}}_{R}\;.

If
h,h^{\prime}\in{\rm End}_{k}(R)
are invariant, so are their composition products
hh^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime}h
; moreover,
h={\rm id}_{R}
is invariant. So, the subset
{\rm Inv}_{k}(R)\subset{\rm End}_{k}(R)
is a
k
-subalgebra for the composition product.
Notice that in (
8.4.3
)
\varepsilon_{R}h\in D={\rm Hom}_{k}(R,k)
, the dual
k
-algebra of
R
. Conversely, for any
d\in D
, the formula

(8.4.4)

\overline{d}=({\rm id}_{R}\otimes d){\mathbb{P}}_{R}=(d\otimes{\rm id}_{R}){\mathbb{P}}_{R}\;,

defines an element
\overline{d}\in{\rm Inv}_{k}(R)
. We conclude with the main result of this introductory section.

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
R
be a cocommutative
k
-coalgebra and let
D
be the dual (commutative)
k
-algebra. The map

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\rm Inv}_{k}(R)

\displaystyle d

\displaystyle\longmapsto\overline{d}

where
\overline{d}
is defined by (
8.4.4
), is a homomorphism of
k
-algebras, for the multiplication in
{\rm Inv}_{k}(R)
induced by the composition product of
{\rm End}_{k}(R)
.

We can apply Theorem
8.5
to any
integration pairing

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}\times{\mathscr{D}}\longrightarrow k\;,

appearing in Proposition
8.4
.
We obtain continuous morphisms

(8.5.1)

\begin{split}{\mathscr{C}}\longrightarrow{\rm Inv}_{k}({\mathscr{D}})\;\;&\mbox{(\,resp.}\;\;{\mathscr{D}}\longrightarrow{\rm Inv}({\mathscr{C}})\;\mbox{)}\;,\\
f\longmapsto\overline{f}\;\;&\mbox{(\,resp.}\;\;\mu\longmapsto\overline{\mu}\;\mbox{)}\;,\end{split}

where
{\rm Inv}_{k}
denotes the group of continuous invariant
k
-linear automorphisms of
{\mathscr{D}}

characterized by

(8.5.2)

\int_{G}h(x)d(\overline{f}(\mu))(x)=\int_{G}h(x)f(x)d\mu(x)\;\;\mbox{(\,resp.}\;\;\int_{G}(\overline{\mu}(f))(x)d\nu(x)=\int_{G}f(x)d(\mu\nu)(x)\;\mbox{)}\;,

for any
h,f\in{\mathscr{C}}
and
\mu,\nu\in{\mathscr{D}}
.

Corollary 8.6
.

For any
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
we have the equality of Dirac masses in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)

(8.6.1)

\delta_{g_{1}}\delta_{g_{2}}=\delta_{g_{1}g_{2}}\;.

Proof.

To verify (
8.6.1
) we consider the duality pairing

\circ:{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)\times{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\longrightarrow k\;,

and observe that the action
\overline{\delta_{g}}
on
f\in{\mathscr{C}}(G,k)
is given by

(\overline{\delta_{g}}(f))(x)=f(gx)\;.

Therefore,

\overline{\delta_{g_{1}g_{2}}}(f)(x)=f(g_{1}g_{2}x)=\overline{\delta_{g_{1}}}(\overline{\delta_{g_{2}}}(f))(x)

so that

\overline{\delta_{g_{1}g_{2}}}=\overline{\delta_{g_{1}}}\overline{\delta_{g_{2}}}

in
{\rm Inv}_{k}({\mathscr{C}}(G,k))
, and therefore, by Theorem
8.5
, we obtain (
8.6.1
).
∎

We now embed the group
k
-algebra
k[G]
in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)
via
g\longmapsto\delta_{g}

and sequence (
6.16.2
) may be completed into

(8.6.2)

k[G]\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}(G,k)

Proposition 8.7
.

1.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}(G,k)
is the completion of the ring
k[G]
in
the
k[G]
-linear topology of uniform convergence on balls of any radius;

2.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(G,k)
is the completion of the ring
k[G]
in the
k[G]
-linear topology of uniform convergence on balls of the same radius;

3.

{\mathscr{D}}(G,k)
is the completion of
k[G]
in the
k[G]
-linear topology of simple convergence on balls.

Proof.

Clear.
∎

In the next sections we will specialize the previous discussion to the case of
G
= the profinite group
({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},+)
, equipped with the
p
-adic topology and
G
= the
td
-group
({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},+)
containing the previous profinite group
({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},+)
as an open subgroup. The role of the ring
k
will be played by the
p
-adic ring
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
or by the discrete field
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
.

9
p
-adic Fourier theory on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

9.1
Mahler-Amice theory on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

We describe here
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
and its weak dual
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
, in case
k={\mathbb{F}}_{p}
or
={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

Notation 9.1
.

We use the notation
\Delta_{a}
(resp.
\delta_{a}
) for the Dirac mass concentrated in
a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with values in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
(resp. in
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
).

We proved in full generality that

1.

{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
= Hopf algebra over
k
of continuous
k
-valued functions with the topology of uniform convergence on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
(if
k={\mathbb{F}}_{p}
this is the discrete topology; if
k={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
it coincides with the
p
-adic topology);

2.

{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
= the weak dual of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
, with weak perfect dual pairing

(9.1.1)

(f,\mu)\longmapsto f\circ\mu=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}f(x)d\mu(x)\;.

Then
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
is a Hopf algebra over
k
and it
is equipped with the
natural topology
,
i.e.
the topology of uniform convergence on balls of equal radius. As a topological ring
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
is linearly topologized and complete.

3.

{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
= the strong dual of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
, with strong perfect dual pairing (
9.2.1
)
Then
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
is a Hopf algebra over
k
and it
is equipped with the
topology of uniform convergence on all balls
. It coincides with
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
, equipped with the
k
-canonical topology.

The case
k={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is the classical case of Mahler and Amice, while the case
k={\mathbb{F}}_{p}
is simpler and follows easily from the classical theory.
So, we now make explicit, following Mahler and Amice, the structure of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
and
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
, for
k={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. We will then point out the simplifications to apply when
k={\mathbb{F}}_{p}
.

Proposition 9.2
.

1.

For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we define
f^{[n]}\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

recursively by

(9.2.1)

f^{[1]}(x)=f(x+1)-f(x)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;f^{[n+1]}=(f^{[n]})^{[1]}\;.

For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, we have

(9.2.2)

f(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}f^{[n]}(0){x\choose n}\;,

where the sequence
n\longmapsto f^{[n]}(0)
converges to
0
. In particular,

(9.2.3)

{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\bigoplus}^{\rm u}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}{x\choose n}={\bigoplus}^{\rm can}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}{x\choose n}\;,

where
(-)^{\rm can}
means completion (of the algebraic direct sum) in the
p
-adic topology.

2.

The coproduct
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathscr{C}}
of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is the continuous
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra morphism such that

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathscr{C}}{x\choose n}=\sum_{i+j=n}{x\widehat{\otimes}1\choose i}{1\widehat{\otimes}x\choose j}\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;,

while the counit
\varepsilon_{\mathscr{C}}
satisfies

\varepsilon_{\mathscr{C}}{x\choose n}=\delta_{n,0}\;.

3.

Let

T=\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{0}\in{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;.

Then

(9.2.4)

{x\choose i}\circ T^{j}=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{x\choose i}dT^{j}(x)=\delta_{i,j}\;,

for any
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
. In particular,
T^{n}\to 0
in
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
as
n\to\infty
.
For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, the expansion (
9.2.2
) coincides with

(9.2.5)

f(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}f(y)dT^{n}(y)\right){x\choose n}

4.

For any
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,

(9.2.6)

\mu=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}T^{n}\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{x\choose n}d\mu(x)\;.

5.

{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is the completion of its subring
{\mathbb{Z}}[T]
with the topology of uniform convergence
on balls of the same radius in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. There is a canonical identification as rings

(9.2.7)

{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]\;.

Proof.

\mathit{1}.

We adapt to the present situation the proof described by Colmez
[
6
, §1.2]
.
Let
B_{N}
be the
p^{N}\times p^{N}
lower-triangular unipotent matrix

B_{N}:=(b_{n,a}={n\choose a})_{n,a=0,1,\dots,p^{N}-1}\in GL(p^{N},{\mathbb{Z}})\;.

Let
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Then, for any
N=0,1,\dots
, we consider the column vectors

F_{N}:=(f(0),f(1),\dots,f(p^{N}-1))^{\bf t}\;\;,\;\;C_{N}:=(a^{(N)}_{0},a^{(N)}_{1},\dots,a^{(N)}_{p^{N}-1})^{\bf t}\;,

where
a^{(N)}\in\{0,1,\dots,p^{N}-1\}
and
C_{N}
is uniquely defined by the condition

B_{N}C_{N}\equiv F_{N}\mod p^{N+1}\;.

Notice that, for any
N
,

(a^{(N+1)}_{0},a^{(N+1)}_{1},\dots,a^{(N+1)}_{p^{N}-1})\equiv(a^{(N)}_{0},a^{(N)}_{1},\dots,a^{(N)}_{p^{N}-1})\mod p^{N+1}

so that, for any
j=0,1,\dots
, the sequence
N\to a^{(N)}_{j}
converges in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. We denote by
a_{j}
its
limit and observe that, for any
N=0,1,2,\dots
,

B_{N}(a_{0},a_{1},\dots,a_{p^{N}-1})^{\bf t}\equiv F_{N}\mod p^{N+1}\;.

We have obtained the
Mahler expansion

(9.2.8)

f(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{j}{x\choose j}\;,

of
f
, which, for any
x=0,1,2,\dots
, is a finite sum.
If
P_{j}(x)
, for
j=0,1,\dots
, denotes the binomial function
x\longmapsto{x\choose j}
,
we have

(9.2.9)

P_{j}^{[h]}=P_{j-h}\;\;\mbox{if}\;\;h\leq j\;,\;\;\mbox{while}\;\;P_{j}^{[h]}=0\;\;\mbox{if}\;\;h>j\;.

Then, for
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
as in (
9.2.8
)

f^{[h]}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{j+h}{x\choose j}

and
f^{[h]}(0)=a_{h}
. Then, for any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, (
9.2.8
)
may be rewritten as (
9.2.2
).

Lemma 9.3
.

For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
let

v_{\inf}(f)=\inf\{v(f(x))\,|\,x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\}\;.

Then there is
h\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

v_{\inf}(f^{[p^{h}]})\geq v_{\inf}(f)+1\;.

Proof.

(of the Lemma) Exercise or see

[
6
, Lemme 1.14]
.
∎

This concludes the proof of
\mathit{1}.

\mathit{2}.

follows from the formula

{x+y\choose n}=\sum_{i+j=n}{x\choose i}{y\choose j}\;.

\mathit{3}.
could be proven via Amice transform
[
6
, §2.3]
but we prefer to base our proof on the previous section
8.3
. We need to check that, for any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}={\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

f^{[n]}=\overline{T^{n}}(f)\;.

This follows from Corollary
8.6
, since
T=\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{0}
and
\mu\longmapsto\overline{\mu}
is a morphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebras. So,

f\circ T^{n}=f^{[n]}(0)\;.

So, from (
9.2.9
),

(9.3.1)

{x\choose i}\circ T^{j}=P_{i}^{[j]}(0)=\delta_{i,j}\;,

for any
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

\mathit{4}.
Formula (
9.2.6
) follows from orthogonality of the subset
\{T^{j}\}_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

to the
topological basis
\{{x\choose i}\}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

\mathit{5}.

Formula (
9.2.6
) establishes a map
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\xrightarrow{\ \ }{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
. On the other hand, for any sequence
n\longmapsto a_{n}
in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
\sum_{n}a_{n}T^{n}
is the continuous linear operator
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
P_{h}\longmapsto a_{h}
,
\forall h\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
The description of the topology of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
as the topology of uniform convergence on balls of a given radius was already in
\mathit{2}
of Proposition
8.7
.
∎

A more explicit description of the topology of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
will be given in the next section.

9.2
The natural topology of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

In this section we prove the two important facts:

1.

as a topological ring,
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
equipped with the
(p,T)
-adic topology.

2.

the topology of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
is induced by the valuation
w

(9.3.2)

w(\sum_{n}a_{n}T^{n})=\min_{n}v_{p}(a_{n})+n\;,

for any
\sum_{n}a_{n}T^{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
.

Notice that
2
follows easily from
1
:

Lemma 9.4
.

The topology induced by the valuation
w
on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[T]
coincides with the
(p,T)
-adic one.

Proof.

We show that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

(p,T)^{n}\subset\{y\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[T]\;:\;w(y)\geq n\;\}\ \subset(p,T)^{n}

We have
w(p^{i}T^{j})=i+j
, so that the first inclusion is clear. If now
w(a_{i}T^{i})\geq n
for some
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
we have
v(a_{i})+i\geq n
but then
a_{i}T^{i}\in(p,T)^{n}
.
∎

We now prove statement
1
above.

Notation 9.5
.

For any
h,\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
let

(9.5.1)

{\mathscr{U}}_{h,\ell}=\{\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;|\;\mu(a+p^{h}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\in p^{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,\;\forall\;a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;\}\subset{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;,

which is easily seen to be an ideal of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. The linear topology of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
having
\{{\mathscr{U}}_{h,\ell}\}_{h,\ell}
as a basis of open ideals is
the topology of
uniform convergence on balls of the same radius
. Since locally constant functions are dense
in
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
this is the
weak topology
of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
i.e.
the topology of simple convergence on the continuous
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-linear dual of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

Proposition 9.6
.

Let us identify
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
with
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
via (
9.2.7
).
Then the topology on
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
of uniform convergence on balls of the same radius coincides with the
(p,T)
-adic topology of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
.
More precisely we have :

(9.6.1)

\begin{split}(p,T)^{p^{N}}&\subseteq\bigcap_{h+\ell=N+1}{\mathscr{U}}_{h,\ell}=\\
(p^{N+1},p^{N}T,p^{N}T^{2}\dots,p^{N}T^{p-2}&,p^{N}T^{p-1},p^{N-1}T^{p},\dots,p^{N-1}T^{p^{2}-1},p^{N-2}T^{p^{2}},\dots\\
\dots,p^{2}T^{p^{N-2}},\dots,p^{2}T^{p^{N-1}-1},&pT^{p^{N-1}},\dots,pT^{p^{N}-1},T^{p^{N}})\subseteq(p,T)^{N+1}\;.\end{split}

Remark 9.7
.

The equality in formula (
9.6.1
) may be more easily remembered as

\bigcap_{h+\ell=N+1}{\mathscr{U}}_{h,\ell+1}=p^{N}(p,T,T^{p}/p,T^{p^{2}}/p^{2},\dots\dots,T^{p^{N-1}}/p^{N-1},T^{p^{N}}/p^{N})\;.

Proof.

We first prove the inclusion
\subseteq
in (
9.6.1
),
i.e.
that

(9.7.1)

(p,T)^{p^{h+\ell}}\subseteq{\mathscr{U}}_{h,\ell+1}\;.

Lemma 9.8
.

T^{p^{h+\ell}}\in{\mathscr{U}}_{h,\ell+1}\;.

Proof.

It suffices to check that for
a\in\{0,1,\dots,p^{h}-1\}
the value

(9.8.1)

\begin{split}&T^{p^{h+\ell}}(a+p^{h}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=(\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{0})^{p^{h+\ell}}(a+p^{h}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\\
(-1)^{p^{h+\ell}}\sum_{i=0}^{p^{h+\ell}}&(-1)^{i}{p^{h+\ell}\choose i}\Delta_{i}(a+p^{h}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\pm\sum_{j=0}^{p^{\ell}}(-1)^{a+jp^{h}}{p^{h+\ell}\choose a+jp^{h}}\end{split}

is
\equiv 0\mod p^{\ell+1}
.
To do so, we recall that

{p^{n+1}\choose pj}\equiv{p^{n}\choose j}\mod p^{n+1}

[
12
, 5.11.3]
, while, obviously,

v_{p}({p^{n+1}\choose i})=n+1\;,

if
(p,i)=1
.
Then, if
a=0
, (
9.8.1
) becomes

\pm\sum_{j=0}^{p^{\ell}}(-1)^{jp^{h}}{p^{h+\ell}\choose jp^{h}}\equiv\pm\sum_{j=0}^{p^{\ell}}(-1)^{jp^{h}}{p^{\ell}\choose j}\equiv 0\mod p^{\ell+1}\;,

if
p
is odd. If
p=2
the better estimate “
\;\equiv 0\mod 2^{2^{\ell}}\;
” holds. If
a\neq 0
, the same sum becomes

\pm\sum_{j=0}^{p^{\ell}-1}(-1)^{a+jp^{h}}{p^{h+\ell}\choose{a+jp^{h}}}\equiv 0\mod p^{\ell+1}\;.

∎

In order to complete the proof of (
9.7.1
) we pick a generator of
(p,T)^{p^{h+\ell}}
of the form
p^{i}T^{p^{h+\ell}-i}
. We may clearly ignore the terms with
i>\ell
. We need to show that for any
0<i\leq\ell

(9.8.2)

v_{p}(T^{p^{h+\ell}-i}(a+p^{h}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\geq\ell+1-i\;.

Since
T({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=0
we may assume
h>0
, so that
p^{h-1}p^{\ell}(p-1)\geq\ell
and then

p^{h+\ell}-i\geq p^{h+\ell-1}\;,\;\forall\;0<i\leq\ell\;.

By Lemma
9.8
,

v_{p}(T^{p^{h+\ell-1}}(a+p^{h}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))\geq\ell\;,

and (
9.8.2
) holds.
So, we proved the first inclusion
\subseteq
in (
9.6.1
).

Let
\mu=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}T^{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
and assume
\mu\in\bigcap_{h+\ell=N}{\mathscr{U}}_{h,\ell+1}
. Equivalently, we assume that

\sum_{n=0}^{p^{N}-1}a_{n}T^{n}\in\bigcap_{h=0}^{N}{\mathscr{U}}_{h,N-h+1}

So, for any
h=0,1,\dots,N
,

(9.8.3)

\sum_{n=0}^{p^{h}-1}a_{n}(-1)^{n}{n\choose a}\in p^{N-h+1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,\;\mbox{for}\;a=0,1,\dots,p^{h}-1\;.

To prove this statement
we apply orthogonality of the binomial coefficient functions and reason by induction on
h
. For
h=0
, we get
a_{0}\in p^{N+1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. We proceed to the case
h=1
where, by the lemma, (
9.8.3
) simplifies into

\sum_{n=1}^{p-1}a_{n}(-1)^{n}{n\choose a}\in p^{N}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,\;\mbox{for}\;a=1,\dots,p-1\;,

which implies that
a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{p-1}\in p^{N}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. The next step,
h=2
, simplifies by the lemma into

\sum_{n=p}^{p^{2}-1}a_{n}(-1)^{n}{n\choose a}\in p^{N-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,\;\mbox{for}\;a=p,\dots,p^{2}-1\;,

and therefore
a_{p},a_{p+1},\dots,a_{p^{2}-1}\in p^{N-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
Proceeding in this way, we find at the
h
-th step the equation

\sum_{n=p^{h-1}}^{p^{h}-1}a_{n}(-1)^{n}{n\choose a}\in p^{N-h+1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,\;\mbox{for}\;a=p^{h-1},p^{h-1}+1,\dots,p^{h}-1\;.

On the other hand, the
p^{N}\times p^{N}
matrix

({n\choose a})_{n,a=0,1,\dots,p^{N}-1}
is unipotent and contains the
N+1
diagonal blocks

({n\choose a})_{n,a=p^{h-1},\dots,p^{h}-1}
, for
h=0,1,\dots,N
, of unipotent
(p^{h}-p^{h-1})\times(p^{h}-p^{h-1})
matrices

(9.8.4)

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccccc }1&0&\dots&\dots&0&0\\
{{p^{h-1}+1}\choose{p^{h-1}}}&1&&\dots&\dots&0\\
{{p^{h-1}+2}\choose{p^{h-1}}}&{{p^{h-1}+2}\choose{p^{h-1}+1}}&\dots&\dots&\dots&0\\
\dots&\dots&\dots&0&\dots&0\\
\dots&{n\choose a}&\dots&1&0&0\\
{{p^{h}-2}\choose{p^{h-1}}}&\dots&\dots&&1&0\\
{{p^{h}-1}\choose{p^{h-1}}}&{{p^{h}-1}\choose{p^{h-1}+1}}&{{p^{h}-1}\choose{p^{h-1}+2}}&\dots&{{p^{h}-1}\choose{p^{h}-2}}&1\\
\end{array}\right)\;.

We conclude
that
a_{i}\in p^{N-h+1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, for
p^{h-1}\leq i\leq p^{h}-1
. So,
\mu
belongs to the ideal

(p^{N+1},p^{N}T,\dots,p^{N}T^{p-1},p^{N-1}T^{p},\dots,p^{N-1}T^{p^{2}-1},p^{N-2}T^{p^{2}},\dots,pT^{p^{N-1}},\dots,pT^{p^{N}-1},T^{p^{N}})\;,

as claimed.
∎

Remark 9.9
.

It follows that the weak topology of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]
coincides with the product topology of the
p
-adic topology on every factor
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}T^{n}
of the representation (
9.2.7
).

Corollary 9.10
.

The coproduct
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathscr{D}}
of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is the continuous
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra morphism such that

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathscr{D}}T=1\widehat{\otimes}T+T\widehat{\otimes}1+T\widehat{\otimes}T\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[T]]\;,

while the counit
\varepsilon_{\mathscr{D}}
satisfies

\varepsilon_{\mathscr{D}}T=0\;.

Corollary 9.11
.

Let

t=\delta_{1}-\delta_{0}\in{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\;.

Then

{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is the completion of its subring
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t]
with the topology of uniform convergence
on balls of the same radius in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
We have

(9.11.1)

(t^{p^{n}})=\{\mu\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t]\,|\,\mu(a+p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=0\,,\,\forall\,a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\,\}

so that, as topological rings,

(9.11.2)

{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})={\mathbb{F}}_{p}[[t]]\;,

equipped with the
t
-adic topology.

Definition 9.12
.

We let
{\mathscr{Q}}_{0}
denote the completion of the field
{\mathbb{Q}}(T)
in the valuation
w
: it is a non-archimedean field extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, with residue field
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
and uniformizer
p
or
T
, that contains
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
as a closed topological subring.

We find it useful to consider the Berkovich analytic line
({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{{\mathscr{Q}}_{0}},x)
, where the coordinate
x
is a formal coordinate on the unit disc of
({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},x)
, and the corresponding theory of
{\mathscr{Q}}_{0}
-analytic functions. From this viewpoint, Proposition
9.2
has the following (obvious) analytic interpretation

Lemma 9.13
.

1.

Let
{\mathscr{R}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
be the ring of
{\mathscr{Q}}_{0}
-entire functions
f
such that
f({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\subset{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.
The character

\Delta_{(-)}:({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},+)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;\;,\;\;x\longmapsto\Delta_{x}

extends analytically as

\Delta_{x}:=\Delta_{1}^{x}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}{x\choose n}T^{n}\in{\mathscr{R}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;.

2.

So, for any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, formula (
9.2.6
) for
f(x)=f\circ\Delta_{x}

is a “Fourier expansion” of
f
w.r.t. a basis
\{{x\choose n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of
p
-adically entire (polynomial!) functions
{x\choose n}\in{\mathbb{Q}}[x]
,
such that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

{a\choose n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;\;,\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;a\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,

and

{x+y\choose n}=\sum_{i+j=n}{x\choose i}{y\choose j}\;.

10
The ring
{\mathscr{D}}={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
of uniform measures on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

10.1
Spaces of functions and measures on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

We review what the general theory of integration on
td
-spaces tells us in the case of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. We have introduced

1.

{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) = ring of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-valued (resp.
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-valued) continuous functions equipped with the topology of uniform convergence on compact subsets of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
;

2.

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) = (non-unital) ring of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-valued (resp.
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-valued) continuous functions “with almost compact support”
i.e.
“vanishing at
\infty
” on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, with the topology of uniform convergence on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
,
i.e.
with the
p
-adic (resp. discrete) topology;

3.

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) = ring of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-valued (resp.
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-valued) uniformly continuous functions on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with the topology of uniform convergence on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
,
i.e.
with the
p
-adic (resp. discrete) topology;

4.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) = weak dual of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
)
(see (
6.9.1
)). It is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-module of maps
\Sigma({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\xrightarrow{\ \mu\ }{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
which satisfy the condition
ACSMEAS
of
\mathit{3}
of Proposition
7.2
equipped with the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-linear topology described there. Similarly for
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
.

5.

{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) = strong dual of
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
),
i.e.
the
p
-adic completion of
\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathscr{D}}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathscr{D}}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
equipped with the discrete topology) (see (
6.16.6
));

6.

{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) = weak dual of
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) =
{\rm lim}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathscr{D}}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(see (
6.10.1
));

7.

{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) = strong dual of
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) =
{\rm lim}^{\square,{\rm u}}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm strong}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(see (
6.10.3
));

8.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) (see (
6.13.9
)) is the set of functions

\mu:\Sigma_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;\;\mbox{(resp. $\longrightarrow{\mathbb{F}}_{p}$)}

such that, for any disjoint family
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
of uniformly measurable subsets of a given radius

\sum_{\alpha\in A}\mu(U_{\alpha})

is an
A
-series unconditionally converging to
\mu(\bigcup_{\alpha\in A}U_{\alpha})
(see
\mathit{2}
of Proposition
7.2
). It is equipped with its
natural topology
,
i.e.
the topology of uniform convergence on uniformly measurable subsets of the same radius.

We consider the sub-semigroup

(10.0.1)

S:={\mathbb{Z}}[1/p]\cap{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}

of
({\mathbb{R}},+)
.

The following result is a particular case of Proposition
8.4
.

Proposition 10.1
.

1.

{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) is the weak dual of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
);

2.

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) is the weak dual of
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
).

3.

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) is the strong dual of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) .

Proposition 10.2
.

1.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is the completion of the group-Hopf-algebra
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
in the
(\delta_{1}-\delta_{0})
-adic topology or equivalently of
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]
in the
t
-adic topology, where
t=\delta_{1}-\delta_{0}
.
In particular, it is a perfect ring.

2.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is also
p
-adically complete and
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/p{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is a perfect ring. Hence

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, as a ring equipped with the
p
-adic filtration, is a strict
p
-ring in the sense of
[
20
, Chap. II, §5]
and, as rings,

(10.2.1)

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\rm W}({\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p}))\;.

3.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
= the completion of the group-algebra
{\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
in the
(p,\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{0})
-adic topology.

4.

For
n=0,1,2,\dots
, let

(10.2.2)

T_{n}=\Delta_{1/p^{n}}-\Delta_{0}\;,

so that, in particular,
T_{0}=T
. We set

(10.2.3)

\widetilde{T}_{n}=\lim_{m\to\infty}(\Delta_{1/p^{n+m}}-\Delta_{0})^{p^{m}}=\lim_{m\to\infty}T_{n+m}^{p^{m}}=[\delta_{1/p^{n}}-\delta_{0}]=[t^{1/p^{n}}]\;,

all
p
-adically convergent expressions.
For
n=1,2,\dots
,
\widetilde{T}_{n}
is the only
p^{n}
-th root of
\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{T}_{0}
in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

and
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
= the completion of its subring
{\mathbb{Z}}[\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{\infty}}]
in the
(p,\widetilde{T})
-adic topology.

5.

Equality (
10.2.1
) also holds topologically in the two cases:

(a)

If
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) is equipped with the
p
-adic (resp. discrete) topology, and the ring of Witt vectors is regarded as a
p
-adic ring;

(b)

If
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
) is equipped with its natural topology,
i.e.
the topology of uniform convergence on uniformly measurable subsets of the same radius, and the ring of Witt vectors is equipped with the product topology.

Proof.

\mathit{1}.

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
is the
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-vector space generated by the symbols
\delta_{a}
, for
a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. The identification of
\delta_{a}
with the
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-valued Dirac mass concentrated in
a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
determines the identification of

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
with a
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-sub-vector space of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
.
Then

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
is also a
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-Hopf-sub-algebra of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, with product
\delta_{a}\delta_{b}=\delta_{a+b}
, for
a,b\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, so that
\delta_{0}
is the identity. The coproduct and counit are given by

{\mathbb{P}}\delta_{a}=\delta_{a}\otimes\delta_{a}\;\;,\;\;\varepsilon\delta_{a}=1\;,\;\forall\;a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\;,

respectively. Moreover,
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
is a perfect ring of characteristic
p
because, for any finite sum
\sum_{a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}c_{a}\delta_{a}
,
c_{a}\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
,

(\sum_{a}c_{a}\delta_{a})^{1/p}=\sum_{a}c_{a}\delta_{a/p}\;.

As we explained in
\mathit{2}
of Proposition
8.7
,
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is the completion of

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
for the
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
-linear topology of uniform convergence on balls of the same radius in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Notice that, for any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, Frobenius of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
establishes isomorphisms of linearly topologized
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebras

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(p^{-i}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})={\mathbb{F}}_{p}[[t^{1/p^{i}}]]\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})={\mathbb{F}}_{p}[[t]]\;,

all equipped with the
t
-adic topology. In fact, by Corollary
9.11
and in particular (
9.11.1
), for any
i,n

(10.2.4)

t^{p^{n}}{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{i}}]=\{\mu\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{i}}]\,|\,\mu(a+p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=0\,,\,\forall\,a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\,\}\;.

By the general theory,

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})={\rm colim}^{\rm u}_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(p^{-i}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})

is the completion of

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]={\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]

in the topology of uniform convergence on balls of given radius. But formula (
10.2.4
) shows that
for any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

t^{p^{n}}{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]=\{\mu\in{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]\,|\,\mu(a+p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=0\,,\,\forall\,a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\,\}\;,

so that
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
coincides with
the
t
-adic completion of

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]={\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]

as claimed.

\mathit{2}.
By the general theory,
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is complete in the topology of uniform convergence on uniformly measurable subsets of the same radius. So it is an object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
.
As recalled in Remark
2.2
, any object of
{\mathcal{C}}{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm u}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
is complete in its
p
-adic topology.
All assertions of this point are then well-known.

\mathit{3}.
We use crucially, for
X={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and
k={\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, the double presentation (
6.16.5
) of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm acs}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
as a limit and as a colimit, and the uniformity
({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},\Theta_{\mathscr{J}})
, where
{\mathscr{J}}:=(\pi_{h})_{h\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is described in Remark
4.33
.
From Proposition
9.6
we know that
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
identifies with the completion of its subring
{\mathbb{Z}}[T]={\mathbb{Z}}[\Delta_{1}]
, where
T=\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{0}
, in the
(p,T)
-adic topology. It follows that
{\mathscr{D}}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
identifies with the completion of its subring
{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{n}]={\mathbb{Z}}[\Delta_{1/p^{n}}]
, for
T_{n}
as in (
10.2.2
). So,

{\mathbb{Z}}[T]\subset{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1}]\subset{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{2}]\subset\dots\;.

From the two relations (
p
is assumed odd; the case
p=2
is analogous)

T_{1}^{p}=T-\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}(-1)^{i}{p\choose i}\Delta_{i/p}\in(p,T){\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1}]\;\;,\;\;T=T_{1}^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}(-1)^{i}{p\choose i}\Delta_{i/p}\in(p,T_{1}^{p}){\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1}]

it follows that

(p,T){\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1}]=(p,T_{1}^{p}){\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1}]=(p,T_{1})^{p}{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1}]\;.

In the ring
{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{\infty}]=\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{n}]
we obtain

(p,T_{0})=(p,T_{1}^{p})=(p,T_{1})^{p}\;.

Hence

(p,T_{1})=(p,T_{2}^{p})=(p,T_{2})^{p}\;.

So, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we have

(10.2.5)

(p,T)=(p,T_{n}^{p^{n}})=(p,T_{n})^{p^{n}}\;.

We conclude that the
(p,T)
-adic topology and the
(p,T_{n})
-adic topology of
{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{\infty}]
coincide for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Both the
(p,T)
-adic topology of
{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{n}]
and the natural topology of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
i.e.
the topology of uniform convergence on balls of the same radius in
p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, are linear topologies, hence

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
with its natural topology coincides with the completion of its subring
{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{n}]
equipped with the
(p,T)
-adic topology.
This remains true for
{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{\infty}]\subset{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and proves
\mathit{3}
.

\mathit{4}.
Clear.

\mathit{5}.
Part
(a)
is well-known. For part
(b)
, we observe that a basis of open neighborhoods of 0 in the product topology of
{\rm W}({\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p}))
consists of the ideals

(p^{N},[t^{p^{N}}])=\sum_{i=0}^{N}p^{i}[t^{p^{N-i}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})]

for
N=0,1,\dots
, and that this system is cofinal with
\{(p,[t])^{N}\}_{N=0,1,\dots}
.
∎

Definition 10.3
.

For
S
as in (
10.0.1
), we define an
S
-valued valuation
w:{\mathbb{Z}}[\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{\infty}}]\to S\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
as

(10.3.1)

w(\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q})=\min_{q\in S}(v_{p}(a_{q})+q)\;.

Obviously,
w
extends to a
{\mathbb{Z}}[1/p]
-valued valuation
w:{\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{\infty}})\to{\mathbb{Z}}[1/p]
. We denote by
{\mathscr{Q}}
the completion of the valued field
({\mathbb{Q}}(\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{\infty}}),w)
.

Then
({\mathscr{Q}},w)
is a non-archimedean
{\mathbb{Z}}[1/p]
-valued field extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with uniformizer
\widetilde{T}
or
p
and residue field
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
.
In particular, Berkovich theory of
{\mathscr{Q}}
-analytic spaces applies.

Lemma 10.4
.

The topology induced by the valuation
w
on
{\mathbb{Z}}[\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{\infty}}]
coincides with the
(p,\widetilde{T})
-adic one.

Proof.

It suffices to show that, for any
n=3,4,\dots
,

(p,\widetilde{T})^{n}\subset\{y\in R\;:\;w(y)\geq n\;\}\ \subset(p,\widetilde{T})^{n-1}

We have
w(p^{i}\widetilde{T}^{j})=i+j
, so that the first inclusion is clear. If now
w(a_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q})\geq n
for some
q\in S
,

v(a_{q})+q\geq n
implies
v(a_{q})+\lfloor q\rfloor\geq n-1
but then
p^{v(a_{q})}\widetilde{T}^{\lfloor q\rfloor}\in(p,\widetilde{T})^{n-1}
.
∎

Corollary 10.5
.

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is the
w
-completion of
{\mathbb{Z}}[\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{\infty}}]
. Then,
every element in

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is the sum of a unique
S
-series
\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q}
, with
a_{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, such that the following condition holds

(10.5.1)

For any
C>0
,
v(a_{q})+q>C

for almost all
q\in S
.

Conversely, any
S
-series
\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q}
such that the map
q\mapsto a_{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
satisfies (
10.5.1
), converges in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
along the filter of cofinite subsets of
S
.

The
(p,\widetilde{T})
-adic topology of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
coincides with the topology induced by the valuation

(10.5.2)

w(\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q})=\min_{q\in S}(v(a_{q})+q)\in S\;,

for any
\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

Definition 10.6
.

We will denote by
{\mathscr{Q}}\{x\}
the
{\mathscr{Q}}
-Fréchet algebra of entire functions on the Berkovich affine line
({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{{\mathscr{Q}}},x)=({\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},x)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{\mathscr{Q}}
. In particular
{\mathscr{D}}:={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is the closure of

{\mathbb{Z}}[\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{\infty}}]
in
{\mathscr{Q}}
. We let
{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be the ring of
{\mathscr{Q}}
-entire functions
f
such that
f({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\subset{\mathscr{D}}
.

10.2
Change of variables in
{\mathscr{D}}={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

We denote by
{\mathscr{D}}
, for short, the ring
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
equipped with its natural topology,
i.e.
the topology of uniform convergence on balls of the same radius. We proved that
{\mathscr{D}}
is the completion of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
in the
(p,T)
-adic topology, for
T=\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{0}
.
We also denote by
{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,
the ring
{\mathscr{D}}
equipped with its
p
-adic topology. Notice that
{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,
is complete
and that it contains properly the
p
-adic completion
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
whose residue ring is
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]
equipped with the discrete topology. We also set
\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}={\mathscr{D}}/p{\mathscr{D}}
(topological quotient) which coincides, for
t=T\mod p
, with the
t
-adic completion of

{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]={\rm colim}({\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t]\xrightarrow{\ \varphi\ }{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[T]\xrightarrow{\ \varphi\ }\dots)\;,

where
\varphi
is Frobenius and is therefore a perfect ring of characteristic
p
.
Notice that the topological quotient

{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,/p{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,=\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}^{\rm dis\,}\;,

which is the ring
\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}
equipped with the discrete topology and contains properly
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[t^{1/p^{\infty}}]
.

The topological ring
{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,
is a strict
p
-ring in the sense of
[
20
, Chap. II, §5]
with residue ring
\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}^{\rm dis\,}
. In particular, the map

(10.6.1)

\theta:{\rm W}(\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}^{\rm dis\,})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,

of
loc. cit.
is an isomorphism of rings. For any
x\in\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}
we simply denote
\theta([x])
by

[x]
. In the terminology of
loc. cit.
, it is the
multiplicative representative
of
x
in
{\mathscr{D}}
.

The reason why we need the
(p,T)
-adic topology and not just the (finer)
p
-adic topology on

{\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]
is that we need a change of variable which would not converge
p
-adically, but only converges
(p,T)
-adically, as we now explain.
Let

E(T)=\exp(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}T^{p^{i}}/p^{i})\in 1+T{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}[[T]]

(similar to
\exp(T)
) be the (
p
-adic)
Artin-Hasse exponential
series and let

L(T)\in T+T^{2}{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}[[T]]

(similar to
\log(1+T)
)
be its inverse in the sense that

E(L(T))=1+T\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;L(E(T)-1)=T\;.

Then
L
is called the (
p
-adic)
Artin-Hasse logarithm
.
The two Artin-Hasse series, the exponential
E(T)
and the logarithm
L(T)
, establish an isomorphism between the formal open
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-disc centered at
0
and the formal open
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-disc centered at
1
. We denote by
\overline{E}(t)\in 1+t{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[[t]]
and
\overline{L}(t)\in t+t^{2}{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[[t]]
the reductions of
E
and
L
modulo
p{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}[[T]]
.

Proposition 10.7
.

1.

Let

\overline{\mu}_{\rm can}:=\overline{L}(t)\in t+t^{2}{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[[t]]\subset{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\;.

2.

The expression

\lim_{n\to+\infty}L(\Delta_{1/p^{n}}-1)^{p^{n}}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}L(T_{n})^{p^{n}}

converges in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
to the element

\mu_{\rm can}:=[\overline{\mu}_{\rm can}]\;.

3.

Conversely, for any
i=0,1,2,\dots
,

\lim_{n\to+\infty}E(\mu_{\rm can}^{1/p^{n+i}})^{p^{n}}=\Delta_{1/p^{i}}

for the
p
-adic topology of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

10.3
Measures on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and Witt vectors

Proposition 10.8
.

1.

The expression
\lim_{n\to+\infty}(\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{n}}+1)^{p^{n}}
converges in
{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,
to the element

\Delta_{1}:=[1+t]\;.

For any
i=1,2,\dots
,
\lim_{n\to+\infty}(\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{n+i}}+1)^{p^{n}}
converges in
{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,
to a definite
p^{i}
-th root
\Delta_{1/p^{i}}=[1+t^{1/p^{i}}]
of
\Delta_{1}
. For any
i=0,1,2,\dots
, we set
\delta_{1/p^{i}}:=\Delta_{1/p^{i}}\mod p\in\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}
, so that

\Delta_{1/p^{i}}=[\delta_{1/p^{i}}]\;.

2.

We have in
{\mathscr{D}}^{p-{\rm adic}}\,

\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{i}}=[t^{1/p^{i}}]=[\delta_{1/p^{i}}-1]=\lim_{n\to+\infty}(\Delta_{1/p^{n+i}}-1)^{p^{n}}\;.

3.

Let

\overline{\mu}_{\rm can}:=\overline{L}(t)\in t+t^{2}{\mathbb{F}}_{p}[[t]]\;.

4.

The two expressions

\lim_{n\to+\infty}L(\Delta_{1/p^{n}}-1)^{p^{n}}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}L(\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{n}})^{p^{n}}

both converge in
{\mathscr{D}}
to the element

\mu_{\rm can}:=[\overline{\mu}_{\rm can}]\;.

5.

Conversely, for any
i=0,1,2,\dots
,

\lim_{n\to+\infty}E(\mu_{\rm can}^{1/p^{n+i}})^{p^{n}}=\Delta_{1/p^{i}}

in
{\mathscr{D}}
.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

Remark 10.9
.

The sum

(10.9.1)

\pi(\widetilde{T})=\sum_{i=-\infty}^{\infty}\widetilde{T}^{p^{i}}/p^{i}

converges in
{\mathscr{D}}
.

Remark 10.10
.

The maps

(10.10.1)

\begin{split}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\ \ }{\mathscr{D}}\;\;,&\;\;s\longmapsto\Delta_{s}\\
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\xrightarrow{\ \ }\widetilde{{\mathscr{D}}}\;\;,&\;\;s\longmapsto\delta_{s}\end{split}

are obviously continuous characters. In fact, for any
s_{1},s_{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

\Delta_{s_{1}+s_{2}}=\Delta_{s_{1}}\Delta_{s_{2}}\;\;,\;\;\delta_{s_{1}+s_{2}}=\delta_{s_{1}}\delta_{s_{2}}\;.

Continuity follows from the fact that, for any
h=0,1,\dots
,
|s_{1}-s_{2}|_{p}\leq p^{-h}
if and only if for any ball
B=a+p^{h}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
of radius
\pi_{h}
,
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
are both in or both out of
B
.

The previous characters are in fact locally
{\mathscr{Q}}
-analytic in a neighborhood of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
in the analytic Berkovich
{\mathscr{Q}}
-line. For any
s_{0}\in p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
the formulas

(10.10.2)

\begin{split}\Delta_{s}=\Delta_{1/p^{n}}^{sp^{n}}=(1+\widetilde{T}^{1/p^{n}})^{sp^{n}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}{sp^{n}\choose i}\widetilde{T}^{i/p^{n}}\\
\delta_{s}=\delta_{1/p^{n}}^{sp^{n}}=(1+t^{1/p^{n}})^{sp^{n}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}{sp^{n}\choose i}t^{i/p^{n}}\end{split}

provide a
{\mathscr{Q}}
-analytic continuation of
s\longmapsto\Delta_{s}
and of
s\longmapsto\delta_{s}
to a neighborhood of
s_{0}
in the Berkovich
{\mathscr{Q}}
-line.

11
Fourier theory for
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})={\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\prime}_{\rm strong}

11.1
Uniformly continuous functions
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

Let
S:={\mathbb{Z}}[1/p]\cap{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
, be as before. For
q\in S
the function
{x\choose q}^{(p)}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is defined as

(11.0.1)

{x\choose q}^{(p)}:=\lim_{n\to+\infty}{{p^{n}x}\choose{p^{n}q}}\;,\;\forall\;x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\;.

The convergence of (
11.0.1
) in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
follows from the congruence

(11.0.2)

{{p\gamma}\choose{p\beta}}\equiv{{\gamma}\choose{\beta}}\mod p\gamma{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,

for
\beta,\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}

[
12
, 5.11.3]
.

Proposition 11.1
.

The functions
{x\choose q}^{(p)}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, for
q\in S
, are continuous.

Proof.

Let
q=p^{-m}Q
, with
m,Q\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Continuity of
x\longmapsto{x\choose q}
may be checked locally : for
x\in p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

{x\choose q}^{(p)}=\lim_{i\to\infty}{p^{m+n+i}x\choose p^{n+i}Q}

obviously a sequence of continuous functions of
x\in p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. Moreover, by (
11.0.2
), we have for
x\in p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

{p^{m+n+i+1}x\choose p^{n+i+1}Q}-{p^{m+n+i}x\choose p^{n+i}Q}\in p^{m+i+1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;,

so that the sequence converges uniformly to
{x\choose q}^{(p)}
on
p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
∎

Lemma 11.2
.

For any
q\in S
and
s\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

v({s\choose q}^{(p)})\geq(p-1)(v(s)-v(q))\;.

Proof.

If
v(s)\leq v(q)
there is nothing to prove. So assume

v(s)>v(q)
. We have

|{s\choose q}^{(p)}|=|{p^{N}s\choose{p^{N}q}}|

for
N>>0
. Let the
p
-adic digit expansions of
p^{N}s
,
p^{N}q
and
p^{N}s-p^{N}q
be

p^{N}s=n_{N+v(s)}p^{N+v(s)}+n_{N+v(s)+1}p^{N+v(s)+1}+\dots\;,

p^{N}q=a_{N+v(q)}p^{N+v(q)}+a_{N+v(q)+1}p^{N+v(q)+1}+\dots\;,

p^{N}s-p^{N}q=b_{N+v(q)}p^{N+v(q)}+b_{N+v(q)+1}p^{N+v(q)+1}+\dots\;.

Therefore, if
s\in S
,

v({s\choose q}^{(p)})=v({p^{N}s\choose{p^{N}q}})\geq(p-1)(v(s)-v(q))\;.

By continuity in
s
this holds for any
s\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with
v(s)>v(q)
.
∎

Corollary 11.3
.

1.

For any
q\in S
and any compact subgroup
C
of
({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},+)

we have

(11.3.1)

{{x+y}\choose q}^{(p)}=\sum_{q_{1}+q_{2}=q}{x\choose q_{1}}^{(p)}{y\choose q_{2}}^{(p)}\;,

for
x,y\in C
, where for
S_{q}=\{(q_{1},q_{2})\in S^{2}\;|\;q_{1}+q_{2}=q\;\}

the
S_{q}
-series in the statement is unconditionally convergent for the topology of uniform convergence on
C\times C
.

2.

In particular, the functions

{x\choose q}^{(p)}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, for
q\in S
, are uniformly continuous.

Proof.

\mathit{1}.
For a given
q\in S
, and any fixed
v\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there are only a finite subset
F_{v}
of
(q_{1},q_{2})\in S_{q}

such that
v(q_{i})\geq v
, for
i=1
or
i=2
. Now
v(x)
has a minumum
M(C)
for
x\in C
. Let us choose
v
to be
M(C)-h
, for
h=1,2,\dots
,
h>>0
. Then for
(q_{1},q_{2})\in S_{q}-F_{v}
we have
v(q_{i})<M(C)-h
, for both
i=1
and
i=2
. Then

v({x\choose q_{1}}^{(p)}{y\choose q_{2}}^{(p)})\geq(p-1)h\;.

\mathit{2}.

For the given
q\in S
, the subset
G_{q}
of those
(q_{1},q_{2})\in S_{q}
such that

v(q_{2})\geq v(q)
is finite. Let
V(q):=\max_{(q_{1},q_{2})\in G_{v(q)}}v(q_{2})
. We choose
\varepsilon_{h}\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
such that

v(\varepsilon_{h})>V(q)+h
, for
h=1,2,\dots
. Then

v({x\choose q_{1}}^{(p)}{\varepsilon_{h}\choose q_{2}}^{(p)})\geq(p-1)h\;,

independent of
x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. We conclude that

v({{x+\varepsilon_{h}}\choose q}^{(p)}-{x\choose q}^{(p)})\geq(p-1)h

independent of
x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, hence the result.
∎

11.2
Orthogonality

Let
\iota(p):{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
denote the map
multiplication by
p
in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. It induces natural Hopf-algebra morphisms

{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\xrightarrow{\ {\iota(p)}^{\ast}\ }{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;\;,\;\;f\longmapsto{\iota(p)}^{\ast}f\;,

where
({\iota(p)}^{\ast}f)(x)=f(px)
,
and

{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\xrightarrow{\ \iota(p)_{\ast}\ }{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\;\;,\;\;\sum_{q}a_{q}\Delta_{q}\longmapsto\sum_{q}a_{q}\Delta_{pq}

such that

\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(\iota(p)^{\ast}f)(x)d\mu(x)=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}f(px)d\mu(x)=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}f(x)d(\iota(p)_{\ast}\mu)(x)\;.

Equivalently,

\iota(p)_{\ast}(T_{n+1})=\iota(p)_{\ast}(\Delta_{1/p^{n+1}}-\Delta_{0})=\Delta_{1/p^{n}}-\Delta_{0}=T_{n}\;.

The previous maps induce morphisms of weak perfect pairings

(11.3.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{C}}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{D}}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{C}}(p^{-n-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{D}}(p^{-n-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota(p)}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm id}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota(p)_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{p^{-n-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}}

such that for any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}(p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)
and
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}(p^{-n-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},k)

(11.3.3)

\int_{p^{-n-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(\iota(p)^{\ast}f)(x)d\mu(x)=\int_{p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}f(x)d(\iota(p)_{\ast}\mu)(x)\;.

Theorem 11.4
.

For any
q,q^{\prime}\in S={\mathbb{Z}}[1/p]_{\geq 0}
, we have

(11.4.1)

{x\choose q}^{(p)}\circ\widetilde{T}^{q^{\prime}}=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{x\choose q}^{(p)}d\widetilde{T}^{q^{\prime}}(x)=\delta_{q,q^{\prime}}\;.

Proof.

We have

\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{x\choose q}^{(p)}d\widetilde{T}^{q^{\prime}}(x)=\lim_{N\to+\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{p^{N}x\choose p^{N}q}^{(p)}dT_{N}^{p^{N}q^{\prime}}(x)=\delta_{p^{N}q,p^{N}q^{\prime}}=\delta_{q,q^{\prime}}\;.

∎

11.3
Fourier expansion in
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

Theorem 11.5
.

1.

For any
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
we have

(11.5.1)

\mu=\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q}\;,

where
a_{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;\forall q\in S
, and, for any
C>0
,

(11.5.2)

v(a_{q})+q>C\;\;,\;\;\forall\;\forall\;q\in S\;.

For any
q\in S
, we have

(11.5.3)

a_{q}=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{x\choose q}^{(p)}d\mu(x)\;.

2.

For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

we have

(11.5.4)

f(x)=\sum_{q\in S}b_{q}{x\choose q}^{(p)}\;,

where
b_{q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\;\forall q\in S
, and, for any
C>0
,

(11.5.5)

v(b_{q})-q>C\;\;,\;\;\forall\;\forall\;q\in S\;.

For any
q\in S
, we have

(11.5.6)

b_{q}=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}f(x)d\widetilde{T}^{q}(x)\;.

Proof.

Part
\mathit{1}
has already been proven. As for part
\mathit{2}
, we know from the general theory that
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is the strong dual of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. For any element
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
we may define
b_{q}
, for any
q\in S
, as in (
11.5.6
). Then, for any
\mu\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
as in (
11.5.1
)

f\circ\mu=\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}(f\circ\widetilde{T}^{q})=\sum_{q\in S}a_{q}b_{q}\;,

so that the
S
-series on the
r.h.s.
must converge unconditionally in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
Therefore, for any
C>0

v(a_{q})+v(b_{q})>C\;\;,\;\;\forall\;\forall\;q\in S\;.

Since
q\longmapsto a_{q}
is any
S
-sequence subject to (
11.5.2
), the
S
-sequence

q\longmapsto b_{q}
must satisfy (
11.5.5
). Now, for any
\nu=\sum_{q\in S}c_{q}\widetilde{T}^{q}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, the
S
-sequence

q\longmapsto c_{q}
satisfies the estimate (
11.5.2
) so that

\sum_{q\in S}b_{q}{x\choose q}^{(p)}\circ\nu=\sum_{q\in S}b_{q}c_{q}

is an
S
-series unconditionally convergent. This shows that
\sum_{q\in S}b_{q}{x\choose q}^{(p)}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and that it equals the original
f
.
∎

11.4
Final remarks

We now pick a system
(\gamma_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of
p^{n}
-roots of
\gamma_{0}\in 1+p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
\gamma_{i+1}^{p}=\gamma_{i}
, for
i=0,1,\dots
, and set

\gamma:=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\dots)=1+\varpi\in 1+{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat\circ\circ}\;.

We write explicitly

(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\dots)=(1,1,1,\dots)+(\varpi_{0},\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2},\dots)

with
\varpi_{i+1}^{p}=\varpi_{i}\in{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\circ\circ}
.
Notice that for any
s\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
,
\gamma^{s}
makes sense in
1+{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat\circ\circ}
as the expression

(\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n+1},\dots)^{p^{n}s}=((1,1,1,\dots)+(\varpi_{n},\varpi_{n+1},\dots))^{p^{n}s}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}{{p^{n}s}\choose i}(\varpi_{n},\varpi_{n+1},\dots)^{i}\;,

for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
p^{n}s\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
The next proposition shows that the additive character

(11.5.7)

\begin{split}{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}&\longrightarrow 1+{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\circ\circ}\\
s&\longmapsto(\gamma^{s})^{\sharp}\end{split}

is uniformly continuous.

Proposition 11.6
.

Let notation be as above.
We have for any
s\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

(11.6.1)

(\gamma^{s})^{\sharp}=\lim_{n\to\infty}(1+\varpi_{n})^{p^{n}s}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}{{p^{n}s}\choose i}\varpi_{n}^{i}=\sum_{q\in S}{s\choose q}^{(p)}(\varpi^{q})^{\sharp}\;,

where the
S
-series converge unconditionally in
{\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\circ})={\mathscr{C}}_{\rm unif}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\widehat{\otimes}\,{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\circ}
.

Proof.

We only need to show that the
S
-series in (
11.6.1
) converges.

In fact, for
q>>0
,

|(\varpi^{q})^{\sharp}|

is very small. On the other hand, assume
s\in S
; if
v(q)<v(s)
, we have

|{s\choose q}^{(p)}|=|{p^{N}s\choose{p^{N}q}}|

for
N>>0
. Therefore the
p
-adic digit expansions of
p^{N}s
,
p^{N}q
and
p^{N}s-p^{N}q
are

p^{N}s=n_{N+v(s)}p^{N+v(s)}+n_{N+v(s)+1}p^{N+v(s)+1}+\dots\;,

p^{N}q=a_{N+v(q)}p^{N+v(q)}+a_{N+v(q)+1}p^{N+v(q)+1}+\dots\;,

p^{N}s-p^{N}q=b_{N+v(q)}p^{N+v(q)}+b_{N+v(q)+1}p^{N+v(q)+1}+\dots\;.

Therefore, if
s\in S
,

v({s\choose q}^{(p)})=v({p^{N}s\choose{p^{N}q}})\geq(p-1)(v(s)-v(q))\;.

So, let
C\subset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be a compact subset. Then
v(x)
has a minumum
M(C)
for
x\in C
.
We conclude that, for any
h=1,2,\dots
, if
v(q)\leq M(C)-h
then

(11.6.2)

v({s\choose q}^{(p)})\geq(p-1)h\;,\;\forall\;s\in C\cap S\;.

By continuity of
s\longmapsto{s\choose q}^{(p)}
the previous inequality holds for any
s\in C
.

So the
S
-series in (
11.6.1
) converges along the filter of cofinite subsets of
S
.
∎
